                                --PROCLAMATION--
          WHEREAS, Thad Cain spent his entire life of 67 years growing up in Lynn Haven.

          WHEREAS, those who knew him would often say that his heart was as vast as his physical
 stature; and

         WHEREAS, m 1976, Thad was a member of Mosley's second-ever graduating class,
 participating in baseball, basketball, and football throughout his high school years; and

         WHEREAS, he held a deep affection for all things related to sports and continued his
 involvement in ball games by volunteering to coach the girls' basketball teams throughout the eighties
 and early nineties, offering transportation when necessary and dedicating himself wholeheartedly to the
 program; and

        WHEREAS, subsequently, over the years, Thad could be seen at every Bay County athletics
 competition, as well as all the Mosley football and basketball games; and

         WHEREAS, the city honored the Cain and Griffin families, who were the original settlers of
 the land, by naming the Cain Griffin Park in Lynn Haven after them; and

         WHEREAS, according to local lore, Cain Griffin Field was considered "Thad's field", as he
 frequently hit the ball out of the park and into the backyards of the houses across the street or onto the
 rooftops; and

        WHEREAS, Thad was also a member of Gents Inc., where he organized the annual golf
 tournaments to raise funds for student scholarships; and

          WHEREAS, Thad Cain cherished his wife and family, was a devoted fan of Mosley, the
 Florida Gators, and any sport involving a ball, Thad is survived by his wife of 34 years, Tocosa, and
 their four children;

          NOW, THEREFORE, I, Mayor Dave Lowery, and the City Commission, on behalf of
 all residents in the City of Lynn Haven, Florida do honor the many contributions made by Thad
 Cain to our City, and hereby proclaim September 24th every year as:

                                   THAD CAIN DAY
        IN WITNESS WHEREOF, I have hereunto set my hand and caused the seal of the City
 of Lynn Haven, Florida to be affixed, this 22nd day of September 2026.




                                                                           Dr. Dave Lowery, Mayor
Attest:



Chris Lightfoot, City Manager

Department: Finance                               Prepared By: Kiki Roman


Agenda Title: A) Discussion and possible approval to adopt Resolution #2026-09-584 to set the
final FY 2027 millage rate of 4.35 mills for purposes of submission on the Certificate of Taxable
Value to the Property Apparaiser's Office pursuant to the TRIM Act. (PUBLIC
COMMENTARY)
(Motion: to adopt resolution 2026-09-584 Final Millage Rate of 4.35mills)

B) Discussion and possible approval to adopt Resolution #2026-09-585 Final General Fund &
Enterprise Funds Budget of $54,494,475 for FY 2027. (PUBLIC COMMENTARY)
(Motion: to adopt Resolution 2026-09-585 Final FY2027 General Fund and Enterprise Funds
Budget in the amount of $54,494,475).


Requested Action: Approve the FY2027 millage rate of 4.35 mills.

I. Report in Brief:

The Truth In Millage (TRIM) Act requires Commission action to approve: A) Resolution #2026-
09-584 adopting the final FY 2027 millage rate of 4.35 mills. B) Resolution # 2026-09-585
adopting the final FY 2027 for fiscal year 2026-2027 in the amount of $54,494,475.


Background:

Setting the final millage rate for FY 2027 is the next required step in setting the final FY 2027
budget.

Recommendation:

The City Manager and the Finance Director have reviewed and recommend approval of millage
and the final FY 2027 budget.

                                   CITY OF LYNN HAVEN, FLORIDA
                                    825 Ohio Avenue | Lynn Haven, FL 32444 | (850) 265-2121 | cityoflynnhaven.com




           Honorable Mayor, City Commissioners, and Residents:



          It is my privilege to present the Fiscal Year 2026–2027 Budget for the City of Lynn Haven. Each
          year, the development of our budget provides an opportunity to look carefully at where we are as a
          City, where we are going, and how we can best serve the people who call Lynn Haven home.

           This year, that responsibility was especially significant.

          The development of this budget was one of the most challenging processes our staff has faced. It
          required difficult conversations, careful decisions, and an honest assessment of what we can
          accomplish with the resources available to us. There were no easy answers to many of the decisions
          before us, and every choice was approached with an understanding that the dollars entrusted to the
          City belong to the people we serve.

          Throughout the process, one priority remained constant: to be responsible stewards of taxpayer
          dollars while continuing to provide the quality services our residents expect and deserve. We
          understand the importance of earning and maintaining the public’s trust, particularly during
          challenging economic times, and that responsibility guides the decisions reflected in this budget.

          We also recognize that the financial pressures facing municipalities are not likely to disappear in a
          single budget year. There may be difficult years ahead. Even so, Lynn Haven will continue to move
          forward. We will continue to look for opportunities, make thoughtful investments, improve the way
          we operate, and provide our residents with the best services and customer service possible.

          I am especially grateful to our City employees. Preparing this budget asked a great deal of our staff.
          They worked through difficult decisions and competing priorities while continuing to carry out the
          day-to-day work of serving our community. Their dedication, professionalism, and commitment to
          Lynn Haven are deeply appreciated. Simply put, we could not accomplish what we do without them.

          Lynn Haven has always been a community that understands how to meet challenges with
          determination and move forward together. I believe that same spirit is reflected in this budget. While
          the decisions behind these pages were not always easy, they were made thoughtfully, responsibly,
          and with the future of our community in mind.

          It is an honor to serve the City of Lynn Haven and its residents, and I remain confident in what we
          can accomplish together.




           Chris Lightfoot
           City Manager



                                                                                                        Mayor Dave Lowery

                                Final FY 2027 Budget ~ 9/22/2026

                                City of Lynn Haven
                                FY 2026-27 Budget
                                Executive Summary

Fiscal year 2027 will have several challenges:

   1) As in fiscal year 2026 General Fund will be paying the debt service of $2.8 million for
      the Hurricane Michael Disaster Recovery bond. This will continue for the life of the
      bond which matures in August 2034. Bond counsel has advised that refunding the
      bond as of this date is not in the City’s best interest. The City will continue to
      monitor the prevailing bond market for any changes that would make refunding
      fiscally attractive.
   2) State Amendment 3 on the November 2026 ballot would reduce the City’s gross
      taxable value by increasing homeowners’ homestead exemption. The current
      estimate for fiscal year 2028 is $1.6 million reduction in Ad Valorem received by the
      City.
   3) The City’s unassigned General Fund balance at the end of fiscal 2026 is estimated to
      be approximately $12 million. Prudent “reserves” for coastal communities is six
      months of operating capital; for Lynn Havn that is $10 million. Which means the City
      will have approximately $2 million to make up the loss of Ad Valorem and to make
      future 2019 Disaster Bond payments.
   4) The method of approval of the millage rate has been changed by the State. A simple
      majority (3-2 vote) of the Commission can approve up to the roll-back rate. A super
      majority (4-1 vote) of the Commission can approve up to 110% of the roll-back rate.
      110% of the roll-back rate or above must have a unanimous vote of the
      Commission. In July 2026 the Commission approved a proposed millage rate of
      4.65 for fiscal year 2027. This is the maximum the Commission can approve in
      September 2026. Different budget models have been based on that millage rate
      and several other rates based on recommendations by the Finance Review
      Committee.

As of September 2nd, all the State’s numbers were available for inclusion in the City’s
revenue calculations.

Given the current financial situation, several cost-saving measures have been
implemented. In aggregate the City will reduce the full-time equivalents by approximately
30 positions. No cost-of-living adjustment (COLA) is included for employees nor




                                  Final FY 2027 Budget ~ 9/22/2026
                                            Page 3 of 107
                                                                                          Page 15 of 207


Commissioners. The cost of medical benefits has been reallocated so that the employees
are paying a comparable amount compared to other nearby municipalities. Total City-wide
payroll expenses will be reduced by $1.69 million compared to fiscal year 2026.

As was the case in fiscal 2026, the City’s Property and Liability insurance has been
reviewed and reallocated. Appropriate amounts have been charged to the CRA, Water
Department, Sewer Department and Sanitation Department. The remaining amount for
General Fund is being recorded in the Administrative Support budget.

Every department has been carefully reviewed for all potential cost savings. The
Community Redevelopment Agency (CRA) has been funded at 75%. Saving the General
Fund approximately $140 thousand. All but three City funded capital projects have been
placed on hold. Grant funded projects remain. The practice of paying employees for
balances exceeding 100 hours of Paid Time Off (PTO) has been terminated. All but the
most crucial travel and training have been removed. City holidays were reduced to save on
overtime. Outsourcing tasks (such as janitorial services) have been reviewed and either
implemented for cost savings or dismissed due to operational concerns. Consolidating
several services with Bay County is being analyzed and discussed. No decisions have been
made as of this writing.

Two full-time positions were moved from Finance to Executive in anticipation of a City
Clerk’s office. No decision has been reached about that office as of this writing.

The Police budget has been reduced by $1.02 million primarily due to staff reductions
which were recommended by the Police Chief.

The Fire Department budget includes $544k for a Station #1 remodel funded by FDEM. No
other capital items are included. Because of this the Fire budget is reduced by $1.02
million.

The Streets Department and Stormwater utility share personnel. These costs will be split
50-50 to more accurately record the staff members’ duties. Streets will recognize $1.46
million in savings due to large projects being completed in FY 2026.

Fleet will purchase an awning to cover the fuel station for $125k which will protect the
equipment and extend its useful life.

Economic Development continues with $3.7 million budgeted for the Florida & Ohio Ave
improvements funded by CDBG.

Sports & Recreation has $123k in savings due to staffing cuts and no capital purchases.




                                  Final FY 2027 Budget ~ 9/22/2026
                                            Page 4 of 107
                                                                                           Page 16 of 207


Parks & Grounds will see a $425k savings from FY 2026 primarily because of staffing cuts
and overall operational cuts.

The Marketing budget has been reduced to less than $25k.

Animal Control is fully funded at $373k.

While receiving 75% funding the CRA will continue to work to increase property values
within its boundaries by investing over $1 million in dedicated funds.

The Water Department will have a budget increase primarily due to $1.6 million meter
purchases required to finish the city-wide AMI installs.

The Sewer Department will save $871k over FY 2026 due to limited capital purchases.

The Stormwater Department budget will be down $6.8 million primarily due to the
completion of the 10th Street Improvement project in FY 2026.

Sanitation will purchase a garbage truck for $680k, while holding the total budget increase
to $53k.

Hurricane Michael Disaster Relief fund will continue to need support from Tetra Tech to file
all the required documents with FEMA, this has been budgeted at $50k.

Surtax money will be utilized for several projects. $2 million will be used for sewer force
main upgrades throughout the city. The total budget of $3.2 will be used to improve the
City’s infrastructure.

To accomplish these goals and objectives, the millage rate will be set at 4.25. A surplus of
$151k will be generated in General Fund.

                       Lynn Haven , Florida
The Area
Located in Bay County in the Florida panhandle, the City of Lynn Haven lies north of
Panama City with its northern boundary just beyond North Bay which connects to St.
Andrews Bay and forms a direct link with the Gulf of Mexico. The current boundaries
encompass approximately 11 square miles with miles of bay front property offering coastal
living with boating, fishing, watersports and sandy beaches.
The City has 12 parks, three of which provide water access including one with a boat
ramp, one with kayak launches, and even a dog park for furry friends. Fishing is also
popular along the old Bailey Bridge in North Bay.




                                Final FY 2027 Budget ~ 9/22/2026
                                          Page 9 of 107
                                                                                     Page 21 of 207


Sports and recreational activities are popular for both youth and adults, providing
opportunities all year long. Indoor and outdoor sports are offered including baseball,
softball, basketball, volleyball, and pickleball.




The area is home to nearby Tyndall Air Force Base and Naval Support Activity Panama
City. Many active duty and retired military members choose to make Lynn Haven their
home because it is a desirable place to live offering a good quality of life.




                               Final FY 2027 Budget ~ 9/22/2026
                                        Page 10 of 107
                                                                                   Page 22 of 207


History
The City of Lynn Haven had its beginning about 1910 as a real estate development with
intentions of providing a haven for Northern Civil War veterans. Promoted by W. H. Lynn,
the town was appropriately named Lynn Haven. After thirty months of construction, Lynn
Haven was incorporated in 1913, and by 1920 had a population of 2,500. The city’s initial
years were marked by rapid growth and construction. However, with the coming of the
Depression, the city's population declined. It was not until World War II and the ship
building industry's surge in Panama City that new, younger families came to the area and
Lynn Haven's population began to increase again. The 1970 census showed a population
of 4,044, increasing to 6,239 in 1980. The census showed a population of 9,298 residents
in 1990, and 12,451 residents in 2000. Today, Lynn Haven’s population is estimated
at 21,733.




                                Final FY 2027 Budget ~ 9/22/2026
                                         Page 11 of 107
                                                                                      Page 23 of 207


Local Government
The City of Lynn Haven has a Commission-Manager form of government. The Mayor and
four Commissioners are elected at large and appoint the City Manager/Clerk who is
responsible for the day-to-day management of the City, and implementation of the policy
decisions of the Mayor and the Commission. The Mayor and Commissioners are
reviewing the role of the City Manager/Clerk in anticipation of a future City Clerk position.

Accounting System
In accordance with Generally Accepted Accounting Principles (GAAP), the City of Lynn
Haven's accounting system is organized and operated on a fund basis. Each fund is
considered a separate accounting entity with resources allocated based on the purposes
for which they are to be used. In effect, the City has several distinct budgets which must
be balanced separately within one operating budget. The development of the budget is
guided by the policies which are contained in state and local laws and those approved by
the City Commission. The budget must be balanced for each fund (Section 166.241 of
Florida Statutes).

Budgetary Process
Each year the Bay County Property Appraiser is required to certify to the City of Lynn
Haven the aggregate taxable value of all property within the City's jurisdiction, as well as
the prior year's tax revenues, for determination of the forthcoming budget and millage
levy. With this information, the City Manager formulates a proposed budget for
presentation to the City Commission no later than thirty days prior to October 1. The
presentation, along with budget workshops, provides the Commission members an
opportunity to review the budget submission to ensure that the proposed budget meets
the best interests of the City of Lynn Haven and its citizens. Before budget adoption, two
public budget hearings are held to present the proposed millage rate and budget as
required by State and Federal law. The budget hearings provide a vehicle for citizen
feedback. The millage rate and budget are adopted by resolution with a majority City
Commission vote, no later than October 1.

Procedures for Property Assessments
Real and personal property valuations are determined each year as of January 1 by the
Bay County Property Appraiser. The Property Appraiser is required to complete his
assessment of the value of all property no later than July 1 of each year. In August of
each year, the Bay County Property Appraiser sends a Truth in Millage (TRIM) Notice to
all property owners as required by law which indicates the assessed property value. The
property owner has the right to file a petition for an appeal with the county Value
Adjustment Board. The TRIM Notice additionally provides information on the proposed
millage rates and taxes of each taxing authority, and states when and where public
meetings will be held to discuss tentative budgets and millage tax rates.

                                  Final FY 2027 Budget ~ 9/22/2026
                                           Page 12 of 107
                                                                                         Page 24 of 207


Revenue Sources
The City's municipal revenues are utilized to pay for the services provided to its citizens.
Revenues collected by the City are taxes, permits & fees, intergovernmental revenues,
charges for services, fines and forfeits, miscellaneous revenue, and other sources. A
general description of major revenue sources follows:

Taxes
Ad Valorem Property Tax - This tax is authorized by Florida statute 166.211 and is levied
on the value of real and tangible personal property. The value of residential property
represents only the value of the real estate, which includes buildings and improvements
while commercial property includes these values in addition to all relevant personal
property. The general millage rate is set by the municipality's governing body and cannot
exceed 10 mills. The Bay County Property Appraiser sets the property values and notifies
property owners with Truth in Millage (TRIM) notices, while Bay County Tax Collector
collects the assessments and remits them to the City for general-purpose government
use.

Local Option Fuel Tax - This tax is authorized by Florida Statute 206.41 and is levied on
every net gallon of motor and diesel fuel sold within the City. The Florida Department of
Revenue administers this tax, collecting and distributing the proceeds which are used for
the construction, reconstruction, and maintenance of roads and streets.
Local Discretionary Sales Surtax - Bay County levies a 1/2 cent Local Government
Infrastructure Surtax which is required to be shared with municipalities effective on
January of 2017. The surtax is collected and distributed by the Florida Department of
Revenue and is used for the paving, repair and maintenance of roads by the City.

Utility Tax - This tax is authorized by Florida Statute 166.231 and is levied on the sale of
electricity and gas, natural or manufactured, within the City.

Communications Services Tax - This tax is authorized for any public purpose by Florida
Statute 202 and is levied on the retail sale of communications services which includes
telecommunications, cable, direct-home satellite and related services. The Florida
Department of Revenue administers these taxes, collecting and distributing the proceeds.
Local Business Tax - This tax is authorized by Florida Statute 205, and represents the
fees charged and the method by which authority is granted for the privilege of engaging
in or managing any business, profession, or occupation within the municipality's
jurisdiction.

Permits & Fees
Permits & Fees - Authorized as Regulatory Fees under Home Rule Authority, these
include various construction permit fees as well as other fees. Revenue estimates are


                                 Final FY 2027 Budget ~ 9/22/2026
                                          Page 13 of 107
                                                                                        Page 25 of 207


based on historical trends and estimated level of construction and are used to fund
services and improvements.

Franchise Fees - Authorized as Proprietary Fees under Home Rule Authority, these fees
are levied on a company or utility for the privilege of using the City's rights-of-way to
conduct utility business. This fee is considered fair rent for the use of such rights-of-way
and consideration for the City's agreement not to provide competing utility services during
the franchise term. This fee is based on a percentage of the receipts from utility sales in
the franchise area and is used to fund General Fund expenditures.

Impact Fees - Authorized as Regulatory Fees under Home Rule Authority, impact fees
represent a total or partial reimbursement to the City for the cost of additional facilities or
services necessary as the result of a new development. Rather than imposing the cost
of these additional facilities or services upon the general public, the purpose of the impact
fees is to shift the capital expense burden of growth from the general public to the
developer or new resident.

Intergovernmental
Grants - These funds are received from various entities, including primarily Federal and
State agencies, to fund in full or in part certain projects.

Shared Revenues - These revenues are comprised of a variety of fees and taxes imposed
and collected primarily by the State of Florida and are shared with municipalities and other
governmental entities. The largest portion of these revenues is derived from Municipal
Revenue Sharing and the Half Cent Sales Tax. These revenues are for General Fund
purposes with a portion of the Municipal Revenue Sharing designated as Municipal
Revenue Sharing Fuel Tax in the City's budget, restricted to construction, maintenance,
and operation of the Street Department. Estimates for these revenues are provided by
the Florida Department of Revenue.


Charges for Services
User Fees - Reflects all revenues stemming from charges for current services. The City's
enterprise funds which include the Water, Sewer, Sanitation, and Stormwater Funds are
supported by user charges. Current rate information and the computation of an average
monthly utility bill for residents are shown below.

 Sewer - base rate $16.95+ $10.17 per 1,000 gallons (Residential only caps at 12,000)

Fines & Forfeits
Fines & Forfeits - Includes revenues received from fines and penalties imposed for the
commission of statutory offenses, violation of lawful administrative rules and regulations,
and for neglect of official duty. Forfeits include revenues resulting from confiscation of
deposits or bonds held as performance guarantees and proceeds from the sale of
contraband property seized by law enforcement agencies.



                                 Final FY 2027 Budget ~ 9/22/2026
                                          Page 15 of 107
                                                                                       Page 27 of 207


Miscellaneous
Interest & Other Earnings - All interest earned on bank accounts and investments.

Rents & Royalties - Rents and percentages of proceeds for use of public property or other
assets.

Other Miscellaneous Revenue - Miscellaneous revenues may be received from a variety
of other non-designated sources that do not fit any of the previously mentioned
categories.

Other Sources
Intragovernmental Transfers In - Transfers between individual funds which are not
repayable and not considered charges for goods or services provided by other funds.

Debt Proceeds - Records the revenue side of installment purchases or capital leases, or
the sale of bonds or other loan proceeds where such proceeds are deposited into
governmental funds.

Other Nonoperating Sources - This includes the sale of property, insurance settlements,
rents, donations and other income not otherwise classified.

Department: CMO                                  Prepared By:


Agenda Title: Approval of Resolution # 2026-09-587 to Relinquish the Florida Resilient Grant
Funding.


Requested Action: Approve Resolution

I. Report in Brief:

The Commission approved the acceptance of the Florida Resilient Grant award for which would
provide for mapping of an east side regional stormwater retention pond. The City also submitted
a legislative request for not only the mapping but also the entire construction drawing and
documents for this project. The City received the legislative appropriation; thus making the grant
award both duplicative and unnecessary. The Resilient Florida Grant Program has asked for a
formal relinquishment of the grant award for their records.


Background:




Recommendation:

Approve the Resolution.

                       RESOLUTION NUMBER 2026-09-587


          A RESOLUTION RESCINDING RESOLUTION NO. 2026-
          04-541 AND DECLINING THE STATE-FUNDED GRANT
          AGREEMENT WITH THE FLORIDA DEPARTMENT OF
          ENVIRONMENTAL      PROTECTION    (FDEP)   FOR
          DEVELOPMENT OF A CITY ADAPTATION PLAN
          RELATING TO THE EFFECT OF SEA LEVEL RISE.

       WHEREAS, on April 14, 2026, the City of Lynn Haven passed and adopted Resolution
No. 2026-04-541 which approved the Standard Grant Agreement 26PLN23 with the State of
Florida Department of Environmental Protection for the City of Lynn Haven Adaptation Plan;

       WHEREAS, since the adoption of Resolution 2026-04-541, the City of Lynn Haven
received funding through an appropriation for the entire project without the need to accept this
Grant;

       WHEREAS, since the Eastside Regional Stormwater Facility is now funded by other
sources, the City of Lynn Haven has no need to accept the Standard Grant Agreement 26PLN23
with the FDEP and the $285,000.00 associated therewith.

      NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED by the City Commission of the City of Lynn
Haven as follows:

       SECTION 1. That the City of Lynn Haven hereby rescinds its approval of the Standard
Grant Agreement 26PLN23 with the FDEP.

       SECTION 2. That the City of Lynn Haven hereby rescinds Resolution No. 2026-04-541.

       SECTION 3. That the City Manager is hereby authorized to contact the Florida
Department of Environmental Protection to withdraw the City’s acceptance of the Standard Grant
Agreement 26PLN23 and execute any necessary documentation to effectuate the declination of
the Grant.

       SECTION 4. This resolution shall become effective upon adoption.

Department: Public Works                           Prepared By: Danny McArdle


Agenda Title: Stormwater Culvert Replacement Change Order for 1102 Vermont Ave


Requested Action: Approval of the Change Order to remove and replace a water service causing
a conflict.

I. Report in Brief:

Staff Recommendations (In motion - ready form): Approval to replace a water service line.
Earlier this year, the commission approved a culvert replacement project at 1102 Vermont Ave.
for Emerald Coast Wastewater Services. This Change Order is to correct a discovered conflict
with a water service line that needed to be moved and replaced to not impede the progress of the
original job.

FY 2026 Budget Impact, 404538-546009 R&M Infrastructure - Budgeted.


Background:

The City has negotiated with Atlantic Pipe Services to perform a large slip lining project to
restore the failing Stormwater culvert along Vermont Ave. The Commission approved Emerald
Coast Wastewater Services, a private contractor, to replace a section of culvert piping that could
not be slip lined by Atlantic Pipe Services. In the process of replacing this section, the contractor
discovered a conflict with a water line service feeding 1102 Vermont Ave that needed to be
addressed. This Change Order addresses the repair and replacement of this conflict.

Recommendation:

Staff is seeking approval of a Change Order in the amount of $ 500.00 for services performed to
remove and replace the conflicting water line.




                                                                                                 Page 124 of 207


II. Budget Amount: N/A                        Project #: N/A



III. Advertised: N/A             Date: N/A                     How/Means: N/A



IV. Alternatives:

The waterline posing the conflict needed to be removed and replaced by the contractor. Choosing
not to have the contractor move the waterline could have caused a delay, charging the city for
additional wait time before city staff could mobilize and do the repair in house.

      _________ ______, by and between the State of Florida Department of Transportation, an agency
            (This date to be entered by DOT only)
       of the State of Florida ("Department"), and City of Lynn Haven ("Recipient").

       NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual benefits to be derived from joint participation on the Project, the Parties
       agree to the following:

               1. Authority: The Department is authorized to enter into this Agreement pursuant to Section 339.12, Florida
                  Statutes. The Recipient by Resolution or other form of official authorization, a copy of which is attached as
                  Exhibit "D" and made a part of this Agreement, has authorized its officers to execute this Agreement on its
                  behalf.

               2. Purpose of Agreement: The purpose of this Agreement is to provide for the Department's participation in
                  Construction of the Tennessee Avenue Sidewalk Project, as further described in Exhibit "A", Project
                  Description and Responsibilities attached to and incorporated in this Agreement ("Project"), to provide
                  Department financial assistance to the Recipient; state the terms and conditions upon which Department funds
                  will be provided; and to set forth the manner in which the Project will be undertaken and completed.

               3. Term of Agreement: The Recipient agrees to complete the Project on or before October 31, 2026. If the
                  Recipient does not complete the Project within this time period, this Agreement will expire on the last day of the
                  scheduled completion as provided in this paragraph unless an extension of the time period is requested by the
                  Recipient and granted in writing by the Department prior to the expiration of this Agreement. Expiration of this
                  Agreement will be considered termination of the Project. The cost of any work performed after the term of this
                  Agreement will not be reimbursed by the Department.

               4. Project Cost:

                    a. The estimated cost of the Project is $ 1,515,426. This amount is based upon the Schedule of Financial
                       Assistance in Exhibit "B", attached to and incorporated in this Agreement. Exhibit "B" may be modified
                       by mutual execution of an amendment as provided for in paragraph 5.i.

                    b. The Department agrees to participate in the Project cost up to the maximum amount of $1,515,426 and as
                       more fully described in Exhibit "B". This amount includes Federal-aid funds which are limited to the actual
                       amount of Federal-aid participation. The Department's participation may be increased or reduced upon
                       determination of the actual bid amounts of the Project by the mutual execution of an amendment. The
                       Recipient agrees to bear all expenses in excess of the total cost of the Project and any deficits incurred in
                       connection with the completion of the Project.

                    c. Project costs eligible for Department participation will be allowed only from the date of this Agreement. It
                       is understood that Department participation in eligible Project costs is subject to:

                       CITY OF LYNN HAVEN, FLORIDA
                       Agenda Item Report Cover
                       THE PUBLIC IS INVITED TO ATTEND




Department: Finance                              Prepared By: Cory Bracy


Agenda Title: Discussion and possible approval of Award of ITB 25.26-20 – Culvert Removal,
Installation, and Site Restoration Services. (City Manager)


Requested Action: To approve the resolution awarding a Master Service Agreement to
Lagniappe Contracting and Paving for culvert removal, installation, and site restoration services
on an as-needed basis in an aggregate amount not to exceed $250,000.00.

I. Report in Brief:

The Public Works Department has a requirement for culvert removal, installation, and site
restoration services on an as-needed basis for routine maintenance and emergency repair needs.
Approval is requested to award a Master Service Agreement to Lagniappe Contracting & Paving
with total expenditures not to exceed $250,000.00.


Background:

The Invitation to Bid (ITB) was advertised on June 10, 2026, on the City of Lynn Haven website
and the City's OpenBids procurement page seeking qualified contractors to provide culvert
removal, installation, and site restoration services. Written questions were accepted through June
18, 2026, and bids were due on July 1, 2026. A total of six (6) responsive bids were received.
Based on the proposed bids, American Sand & Asphalt Paving submitted the lowest responsive
and responsible bid. Staff determined that American Sand & Asphalt Paving was a responsible
contractor capable of performing the required services and the commission reviewed and
approved Resolution 2026-07-571. The recommended awardee, American Sand & Asphalt
Paving, was provided written notice of the intent to award and was requested to execute and
return the required contract documents by a specified deadline. American Sand & Asphalt
Paving did not respond to the City's request or return the executed documents by the deadline.
Therefore, the City will proceed with the next lowest responsive and responsible bidder,
Lagniappe Contracting & Paving, subject to commission review/approval.

Recommendation:




                                                                                              Page 160 of 207


Staff recommends award of the Master Service Agreement to Lagniappe Contracting and Paving
for culvert removal, installation, and site restoration services on an as-needed basis, with total
expenditures not to exceed $250,000.00. Funding is included within the current fiscal year
budget, and future expenditures will be subject to annual appropriation.


II. Budget Amount: $250,000.00                  Project #: N/A



III. Advertised: Yes              Date: 06/10/2026               How/Means: COLH Website,
                                                                 Bay County Column,
                                                                 OpenBids


IV. Alternatives:

None.


V. Attachments:

signed, ITB Bid Tabulation_25.26-20 Culvert Install Services, Updated Bid Breakdown

                        RESOLUTION 2026-09-586

      A RESOLUTION OF THE CITY OF LYNN HAVEN, FLORIDA,
      APPROVING A MASTER SERVICES AGREEMENT WITH
      LAGNIAPPE CONTRACTING AND PAVING FOR CULVERT
      REMOVAL, INSTALLATION AND SITE RESTORATION
      SERVICES IN AN AMOUNT NOT TO EXCEED $250,000.

       BE IT RESOLVED by the City Commission of the City of Lynn Haven
that the appropriate Officers of the City are authorized to execute and deliver
on behalf of the City that Master Services Agreement between the City and
Lagniappe Contracting and Paving, relating to culvert removal, installation
and site restoration services, on an as-needed basis and in an amount not
to exceed Two Hundred Fifty Thousand Dollars ($250,000), in substantially
the form attached and presented to the Council today, with such changes,
insertions or omissions as may be approved by the Mayor and City Manager
and whose execution of such Agreement shall be conclusive evidence of
such approval.

      THIS RESOLUTION shall be effective immediately upon passage.

Agenda Title: 18th Street Paving Project Change Order #1


Requested Action: To approve the change Order in the amount of $19,750 to Gulf Coast Utility
Contractors, LLC for the paving of 11th Street.

I. Report in Brief:

The Change Order for the 18th Street Paving project is for the addition of a portion of 11th Street
that was not part of the 10th Street CDBG project. This will complete the paving on 11th Street
from Tennessee Avenue to Florida Avenue.


Background:

In the initial approval of the 10th Street CDBG project, the portion of 11th Street from the
railroad tracks to Tennessee Avenue was not included. The 18th Street Paving project presented
the opportunity to have this section paved while still being under budget and having a price a
current contractor under contract to perform this work.

Recommendation:

To approve the change order for the completion of 11th Street roadway.

                                                                                       Page 198 of 207


                       CITY OF LYNN HAVEN, FLORIDA
                       Agenda Item Report Cover
                       THE PUBLIC IS INVITED TO ATTEND




Department: CMO                                  Prepared By: JENNIFER HODGES


Agenda Title: Discussion and possible approval of a hiring process for the City Clerk-Treasurer
position.


Requested Action: Approval of position description for City Clerk-Treasurer.
Approval of a selection process for City Clerk-Treasurer.
Selection of a commissioner or the mayor-commissioner to work with the selected applicant and
city attorney to draft an employment contract.

I. Report in Brief:

The City Commission has discussed several times in the past the need to separate responsibilities
and duties of the City Manager/City Clerk, providing for the financial accounting of the city's
revenues and expenditures.


Background:

Based on the desire of the Commission to see if voters also agreed with the creation of a City
Clerk position, the Charter Review Committee recommended ballot language for the creation of
a City Clerk position. The referendum to add a charter position of City Clerk that reports to the
City Commission was passed by the voters at the August 2026 primary election. The City
Commission then passed a motion to create a City Clerk-Treasurer position at its September 8,
2026, meeting. The City Commission needs to review and approve the position description and
determine the process they elect to follow to select the City Clerk-Treasurer.

Recommendation:


    1. Approved the position description for City Clerk-Treasurer.
    2. Decide the selection process to be used by the City Commission for the City Clerk-
       Treasurer position.
           1. Appoint an individual to the position of City Clerk-Treasurer to enter into an
              employment contract for the position of City Clerk-Treasurer, OR




                                                                                               Page 199 of 207


              2. Conduct an internal selection process, allowing internal candidates (i.e.,
                 employees of the City of Lynn Haven) to apply for the position. The Commission
                 will then review the applicants' qualifications and professional experience to
                 determine who, if any, applicant(s) will be interviewed for the position. The
                 Commission will then decide if interviews are needed and, if so, who will be
                 interviewed. Finally, the Commission shall select an applicant to enter into
                 contract negotiations for the position of City Clerk-Treasurer, OR
              3. Conduct an open selection process, requiring a nationwide search for qualified
                 applicants. The Commission will then review the applicants' qualifications and
                 professional experience to determine who, if any, applicant(s) will be interviewed
                 for the position. The Commission will then decide if interviews are needed and, if
                 so, who will be interviewed. Finally, the Commission shall select an applicant to
                 enter into contract negotiations for the position of City Clerk-Treasurer.
      3. Select a Commissioner or Mayor-Commissioner to work with the city attorney and the
         selected applicant for the position of City Clerk-Treasurer to negotiate an employment
         contract.

                                                                    CITY CLERK-Treasurer
                                                                               City of Lynn Haven




NATURE OF WORK
The City Clerk, appointed by and a direct report to the City Commission, is responsible for
directing the City Clerk’s Office which covers the records management responsibilities for the
city, the Finance Department and its related treasury duties, and Utility and miscellaneous
billing for the city. The Clerk or designee provides administrative support to the City Commission
and coordinates statutory and administrative requirements associated with Commission
business. also coordinates the day-to-day business of elected officials and manages statutory
requirements for the Commission. Work is performed as the direction of the City Commission
and in cooperation with the City Manager in matters involving city administration.

EXAMPLES OF DUTIES
The following list of duties is typical for this classification. Incumbents may not perform all
the listed duties and/or may be required to perform additional or different duties from
those set forth below to address city needs and changes with business practices.

City Clerk’s Office and Records Management Responsibilities:
• Serves as the City's official records custodian and oversees the City's records management
    program, including but not limited to ordinances, resolutions, proclamations, contracts,
    agreements, leases, deeds, agendas, meeting minutes, and other official records.
• Maintain all records in a complete, orderly, and accessible manner ensuring the proper
    retention, retrieval and destruction of records according to all legal requirements.
• Prepare meeting agendas and agenda packets for the use by the members of the city
    commission, or other city-related commissions or committees, and provide access to such
    documents to the public.
• Prepare or assist with the preparation of, updates to, and the numbering of resolutions
    and ordinances as requested by the City Commission.
• Ensure the city’s legal responsibilities for posting public notices, legal advertisements, and
    other notices are executed properly, meeting legal deadlines in accordance with Florida
    Statutes and/or City Code of Ordinances.
• Attend all City Commission Meetings and other such Commission-related or Commission-
    appointed commission and committee meetings as Secretary, announcing resolutions,
    maintaining the roll, calling the roll, and recording responses.
• Act as the official custodian of the seal of the City.




                                                                                             Page 201 of 207


•   Prepare correspondences in a professional manner as directed by the City Commission or
    as needed.
•   Coordinate municipal election processes with the Bay County Supervisor of Elections as
    required by law.
•   Respond to and appropriately complete all public records requests as required by Florida
    law.

City Clerk’s Office and Treasurer Responsibilities:

•   Establishes sound financial management strategies for the city.
•   Develops and manages the budget for the City Clerk-Treasurer’s Office budget and
    provides financial oversight and monitoring of the City budget as prepared by the City
    Manager and adopted by the City Commission.
•   Determines cash needs and ensures the adequacy of cash reserves.
•   Ensures the City’s debt programs comply with bond covenants and applicable state and
    federal regulations.
•   Ensures internal controls and procedures for proper collection, processing, and depositing
    of city monies exist and are appropriately employed.
•   Issue all warrants for payment, receive and collect all money belonging to the city, and
    keep an accurate account of all receipts, disbursements, assets, and liabilities of the city.
•   Establishes and maintains appropriate backup authorization and succession procedures
    for financial approvals, warrants for payment, banking transactions, and other essential
    treasury functions to ensure continuity of City operations during the absence, disability,
    vacancy, or unavailability of the City Clerk/Treasurer. Such procedures shall maintain
    appropriate segregation of duties and internal controls.
•   Establishes continuity of operations so that essential financial functions are not dependent
    upon a single individual. Backup authority should be established in advance while
    maintaining appropriate segregation of duties and internal controls.
•   Attends all bid openings and independently verifies that required procurement procedures
    were followed and that sufficient funds are available prior to award and reports such
    verification to the City Commission.
•   Certifies, as required by City policy, that sufficient funds have been appropriated and are
    available before the City enters into a contract, agreement, or other financial obligation or
    authorizes an expenditure.
•   Ensures mechanisms and internal controls are in place for the proper billing and collection
    of all city utility services, and miscellaneous charges as set by ordinance and the city’s fee
    schedule.
•   Provides the City Commission with regular financial reports regarding revenues,
    expenditures, budget-to-actual performance, cash position, reserves, debt, and other
    significant matters affecting the City's financial condition.
•   Coordinates the City's annual independent financial audit, ensures requested financial
    records and information are provided to the City's external auditors, and reports
    significant audit findings and corrective actions to the City Commission.




                                                                                               Page 202 of 207


City Clerk’s Office and General Responsibilities:

•   Demonstrate good working relationships and excellent customer service with the Mayor,
    Commissioners, City Manager, City Attorney, city staff, business and community leaders,
    and interested parties displaying professionalism and honesty.
•   Supervise the operation of the Office of the City Clerk by assigning work and establishing
    work schedules, directing and supervising duties of staff, and reviewing job performance.
•   Perform other related duties as assigned by the City Commission or as required by Charter,
    ordinance, resolution, or applicable law.

EDUCATION AND EXPERIENCE
Education and training shall require at a minimum a bachelor’s degree in accounting, finance, or
a related business area and ten years of administrative and managerial experience in financial
management, seven of which is in the public sector financial management area. Five years of the
required experience must have been in a supervisory capacity. Preferred is an active certification
as a Chartered Financial Analyst (CFA), Certified Public Finance Administrator (CPFA), OR Certified
Public Accountant (CPA) along with a certification as a Municipal Clerk through the International
Institute of Municipal Clerks.


QUALIFICATIONS
The following list describes the knowledge and ability required to perform the expected tasks.

Knowledge of:
• Several professional disciplines, including government accounting, financial analysis,
   revenue collections, risk management and insurance, asset management, debt recovery and
   management, and records management and retention.
• Federal and state legislation and regulations related to such practices and disciplines.
• Principles of modern governmental accounting and municipal financial management.
• The organization, function, and financial challenges of municipal government.
• Internal controls necessary for the collection and protection of city funds.
• Fiscal investment and financial and statistical analysis techniques.
• Florida Ethics Law, Florida Public Records Law, and Roberts Rule of Order.
• Advanced principles and practices of organization, management, and supervision.
• Principles of effective public relations and interrelationships with community groups and
   agencies, private businesses and firms, and other levels of government.

Ability to:
• Provide effective leadership and coordinate the activities associated with the Office of the
    City Clerk.
• Analyze and interpret financial reports and technical data.




                                                                                              Page 203 of 207


•   Analyze problems and exercise critical thinking and professional judgment to arrive at logical
    and valid conclusions.
•   Employ computer software, applications, and programs to effectively operates as a City
    Clerk.
•   Communicate honestly, professionally, and effectively with a variety of individuals and
    groups.
•   Prepare written and oral communications and relational skills.
•   Operate various office equipment, including but not limited to computer terminals, printers,
    calculators, adding machines, facsimile machines, telephones, and other equipment
    typically found in an office setting.
•   Plan, organize, direct, and coordinate subordinate staff, as necessary.
•   Select, supervise, train, and evaluate staff, as necessary.
•   Prepare clear and concise administrative reports.
•   Interpret and apply federal, state and local policies, laws, and regulations.
•   Make effective public presentations.
•   Delegate authority and responsibility as appropriate.

SPECIAL REQUIREMENTS

•   Possess a valid Florida Driver’s License.
•   File a financial disclosure with the Supervisor of Elections in county of residence, in
    compliance with Chapter 112, Part III, Florida Statues within 20 days of placement in this
    position, and annually thereafter.
•   Complete and successfully pass a Level II background investigation.

FLSA Exempt.

PHYSICAL DEMANDS

Work is conducted primarily in an office setting. Work may involve some travel and frequent
attendance at meetings, including many that may be conducted during the evening or on
weekends, and irregular hours as necessary to meet deadlines and achieve objectives.
Physical demands require sufficient physical ability and mobility to work in an office setting; to
stand or sit for prolonged periods of time; to occasionally stoop, bend, kneel, crouch, reach, ad
twist; to lift, carry, push, and/or pull light to moderate amounts of weight; to operate office

equipment requiring repetitive hand movement and fine coordination including use of a
computer keyboard; and to verbally communicate to exchange information.
These are intended only as illustrations of several types of work performed. The omission of
specific duties does not exclude them from the position if the work is similar, related, or a logical
assignment to the position.
